Replace the whole of `src/surface/treeSites.ts` with:

```ts
import { mulberry32 } from '../core/random';
import { WORLD_HALF } from './terrain';
import { LOOP, LOOP_MARGIN } from './wormPaths';
import { scaleSpec, type ScaleSpec, type TreeLayout } from './worldScale';

export interface TreeSite {
  x: number;
  z: number;
  birch: boolean;
  /** A paper birch clump: a second, smaller stem leans from beside the first (forestTrees.ts). */
  clump: boolean;
}

/** These sites (by index) grow as a birch clump. */
export const CLUMPS: ReadonlySet<number> = new Set([2, 12]);

/** A Classic trunk's radius above its root flare: paper birch and sugar maple. */
export const TRUNK_RADIUS = { birch: 0.32, maple: 0.48 } as const;
/**
 * The widest a Classic trunk gets near the ground (its root flare and buttresses, up to the worm's
 * height), measured from the drawn trunks: what the worm and the hero leaves cannot pass through
 * (obstacles.ts). A clump's circle holds both stems: the second stands 0.5 away, at 0.8 the size.
 */
export const TRUNK_FOOT = { birch: 0.52, maple: 1.08, clump: 0.5 + 0.8 * 0.52 + 0.03 } as const;

/** A tree's radius above its flare in `spec`'s mode (trunk shadows, litter drifts). */
export function trunkRadiusOf(t: TreeSite, spec: ScaleSpec = scaleSpec()): number {
  return t.birch ? TRUNK_RADIUS.birch * spec.birch : TRUNK_RADIUS.maple * spec.maple;
}

/** A tree's footprint at the ground in `spec`'s mode. */
export function trunkFootOf(t: TreeSite, spec: ScaleSpec = scaleSpec()): number {
  if (!t.birch) return TRUNK_FOOT.maple * spec.maple;
  return (t.clump ? TRUNK_FOOT.clump : TRUNK_FOOT.birch) * spec.birch;
}

/**
 * Near-tree positions (pure): `count` sites from `seed`, birch and maple in turn, at least `clear`
 * from the spawn and with their footprint (plus a body width) off the speed test's loop. Classic's
 * layout (14, seed 7, clear 4) is the Step 1a forest. The bigger modes keep the spawn's straight
 * run clear by `clear` alone (tests/unit/layout.test.ts).
 */
export function placeTrees(layout: TreeLayout, spec: ScaleSpec): TreeSite[] {
  const rng = mulberry32(layout.seed);
  const out: TreeSite[] = [];
  for (let i = 0; i < layout.count; i++) {
    const birch = i % 2 === 0;
    const clump = CLUMPS.has(i);
    const foot = trunkFootOf({ x: 0, z: 0, birch, clump }, spec);
    let x = 0;
    let z = 0;
    do {
      x = (rng() * 2 - 1) * (WORLD_HALF - 2);
      z = (rng() * 2 - 1) * (WORLD_HALF - 2);
    } while (Math.hypot(x, z) < layout.clear || Math.hypot(x - LOOP.x, z - LOOP.z) < LOOP.r + LOOP_MARGIN + foot);
    out.push({ x, z, birch, clump });
  }
  return out;
}

let cache: { spec: ScaleSpec; sites: readonly TreeSite[] } | null = null;

/** The active mode's near trees (built once per mode). Read-only. */
export function treeSites(): readonly TreeSite[] {
  const spec = scaleSpec();
  if (cache?.spec !== spec) cache = { spec, sites: placeTrees(spec.trees, spec) };
  return cache.sites;
}
```

Create `src/surface/wormPaths.ts`:

```ts
import { AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE, LOCOMOTION } from '../game/drive';

/**
 * The worm's fixed paths, kept clear of solid props and clutter in every scale mode (pure):
 * - the speed test's auto-drive loop: AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE with LOCOMOTION (move {x: 0.35, y: 1} at
 *   speed 1.6 and turn rate 2.2) traces a circle of radius 1.6 / 0.77 ~ 2.08, centred left of the
 *   spawn;
 * - the straight run ahead of the spawn (it is not empty: a climbable windblown leaf pile sits
 *   ahead).
 */
const LOOP_RADIUS = (LOCOMOTION.speed * AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE.move.y) / (LOCOMOTION.turnRate * AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE.move.x);
export const LOOP = { x: -LOOP_RADIUS, z: 0, r: LOOP_RADIUS };
/** Clearance around the loop line: the body, its shadow, and the camera 2.6 behind the head. */
export const LOOP_MARGIN = 1.8;
export const CORRIDOR = { x0: -1.8, x1: 1.8, z0: -3.5, z1: 7.5 };

/** True when a thing of `radius` at (x, z) keeps off the auto-drive loop and the spawn corridor. */
export function clearOfPaths(x: number, z: number, radius: number): boolean {
  if (Math.hypot(x - LOOP.x, z - LOOP.z) < LOOP.r + LOOP_MARGIN + radius) return false;
  return !(x > CORRIDOR.x0 - radius && x < CORRIDOR.x1 + radius && z > CORRIDOR.z0 - radius && z < CORRIDOR.z1 + radius);
}
```

### Task 7: Clutter, trunks, bark, the log and the distant ring at the mode's size (rendering)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/surface/distantTrees.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/forestTrees.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/groundProps.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/propGeometry.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/trunkGeometry.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/trunkShape.ts`
- Create: `tests/unit/scaledProps.test.ts`

The geometry writers take a size factor (`grow`) that draws no random numbers, so Classic's meshes are unchanged (the fingerprint test pins them):
- mushrooms ×3, ferns and sedge ×7 (Hybrid and True);
- trunks: girth, height, lean, limbs, peeling curls, root collar and crown all scale with the species' factor (True birch ×9, maple ×11; Hybrid ×4.5), and the bark pattern is laid out in Classic metres (`aBark.y / grow`), so a big trunk or log wears the same bark, bigger;
- the distant ring: fewer, bigger trees (Hybrid 24 at ×4.5, True 14 at ×9).

The new test also checks that every drawn trunk, log and rock vertex up to the worm's height lies inside its solid footprint, in every mode, which is what makes Task 8's collisions honest.

```ts
/** Length, sum and a weighted sum of an array: a cheap fingerprint of a mesh. */
function fingerprint(a: ArrayLike<number>): number[] {
  let s = 0;
  let w = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < a.length; i++) {
    s += a[i];
    w += a[i] * ((i % 7) + 1);
  }
  return [a.length, s, w];
}
```

with:

```ts
import { MeshWriter, icosphere } from './meshWriter';
import { terrainHeight } from './terrain';
import { treeSites } from './treeSites';
import { scaleSpec } from './worldScale';
import { trunkRadius } from './trunkShape';
import { BARK, writeCurl, writeLimb, writeTrunk, type TrunkSpec } from './trunkGeometry';

const BIRCH_CROWN = ['#e8b830', '#d9a020', '#f0c848', '#c89a28', '#b8a83a'];
const MAPLE_CROWN = ['#e0602a', '#d4402a', '#ec8a2a', '#c83a24', '#e89a30'];

/**
 * One stem: `scale` makes a clump's second stem smaller; `grow` is the scale mode's size for the
 * species (worldScale.ts), applied to girth, height, lean and the root collar alike.
 */
function trunkSpec(x: number, z: number, birch: boolean, rng: () => number, scale = 1, grow = 1): TrunkSpec {
  const lean = (birch ? 0.9 : 0.45) * rng() * scale * grow;
  const la = rng() * Math.PI * 2;
  const collar = 0.4 * grow;
  return {
    x, z, ground: terrainHeight(x, z),
    params: birch
      ? { height: (11 + rng() * 3) * scale * grow, radius: (0.24 + rng() * 0.09) * scale * grow, taper: 0.45, flare: 0.45, buttress: 0.3, lobes: 4, phase: rng() * 6, collar }
```

```ts
}

/**
 * Near forest: paper birches (some in clumps) and sugar maples with bark-detailed, flared,
 * leaning trunks and limbs (one merged draw), and soft autumn crowns (one merged draw).
 * `addBark` lets other props (the fallen log) share the trunk draw.
 */
export function buildForestTrees(addBark?: (w: MeshWriter) => void): THREE.Group {
  const bark = new MeshWriter('aBark', 4);
  const crowns = new MeshWriter('aCanopy', 4);
  const rng = mulberry32(77);
  treeSites().forEach(({ x, z, birch }, i) => {
    const stems = [trunkSpec(x, z, birch, rng)];
    if (CLUMPS.has(i)) {
      const a = rng() * Math.PI * 2;
      const s = trunkSpec(x + Math.cos(a) * 0.5, z + Math.sin(a) * 0.5, true, rng, 0.8);
      s.leanX = Math.cos(a) * 1.4;
      s.leanZ = Math.sin(a) * 1.4;
      stems.push(s);
    }
    for (const t of stems) {
      writeTrunk(bark, t);
      writeDetails(bark, t, birch, rng);
      const H = t.params.height;
      writeCrown(crowns, t.x + t.leanX, t.ground + H * 0.86, t.z + t.leanZ, birch ? 2.6 : 3.3, birch, rng);
    }
  });
  addBark?.(bark);
```

with:

```ts
}

/**
 * Near forest of the active scale mode: paper birches (some in clumps) and sugar maples with
 * bark-detailed, flared, leaning trunks and limbs (one merged draw), and soft autumn crowns (one
 * merged draw). `addBark` lets other props (the fallen log) share the trunk draw.
 */
export function buildForestTrees(addBark?: (w: MeshWriter) => void): THREE.Group {
  const spec = scaleSpec();
  const bark = new MeshWriter('aBark', 4);
  const crowns = new MeshWriter('aCanopy', 4);
  const rng = mulberry32(77);
  treeSites().forEach(({ x, z, birch, clump }) => {
    const grow = birch ? spec.birch : spec.maple;
    const stems = [trunkSpec(x, z, birch, rng, 1, grow)];
    if (clump) {
      const a = rng() * Math.PI * 2;
      const s = trunkSpec(x + Math.cos(a) * 0.5 * grow, z + Math.sin(a) * 0.5 * grow, true, rng, 0.8, grow);
      s.leanX = Math.cos(a) * 1.4 * grow;
      s.leanZ = Math.sin(a) * 1.4 * grow;
      stems.push(s);
    }
    for (const t of stems) {
      writeTrunk(bark, t);
      writeDetails(bark, t, birch, rng, grow);
      const H = t.params.height;
      writeCrown(crowns, t.x + t.leanX, t.ground + H * 0.86, t.z + t.leanZ, (birch ? 2.6 : 3.3) * grow, birch, rng);
    }
  });
  addBark?.(bark);
```

```ts
import * as THREE from 'three';
import { createPropMaterial } from '../render/propMaterial';
import { MeshWriter } from './meshWriter';
import { clutterGrow, heroLog, rocks, placeClutter, type ClutterItem } from './propPlacement';
import { writeFern, writeMushrooms, writeRock, writeSedge } from './propGeometry';
import { meshGroundHeight } from './terrainMesh';
import { terrainHeight } from './terrain';
import { makeShadeAt } from './treeShadows';
import { sharedLighting } from '../render/paintedMaterial';
import { BARK, writeLog } from './trunkGeometry';
import { scaleSpec } from './worldScale';

/**
 * One clutter item, at the active scale mode's size. Clutter rests on the terrain mesh exactly as
 * drawn (soil plus litter mound), so the mound under deep piles never buries it; rocks and the log
 * stay on the soil.
 */
export function writeClutterItem(w: MeshWriter, c: ClutterItem, rng: () => number): void {
  const grow = clutterGrow(c.kind);
  if (c.kind === 'mushrooms') writeMushrooms(w, c.x, c.z, c.scale, rng, meshGroundHeight, grow);
  else if (c.kind === 'sedge') writeSedge(w, c.x, c.z, c.scale, rng, meshGroundHeight, grow);
  else writeFern(w, c.x, c.z, c.scale, rng, meshGroundHeight, grow);
}
```

```ts
const HONEY = [new THREE.Color('#c8954a'), new THREE.Color('#6e4422')];
const RUSSULA = [new THREE.Color('#c0392b'), new THREE.Color('#7a2018')];

/** A cluster of 3-5 mushrooms (honey mushrooms, or a red russula pair). */
export function writeMushrooms(
  w: MeshWriter, x: number, z: number, scale: number, rng: () => number, groundAt: GroundAt = terrainHeight,
): void {
  const red = rng() < 0.3;
  const n = red ? 2 : 3 + Math.floor(rng() * 3);
  const [cap, centre] = red ? RUSSULA : HONEY;
  for (let k = 0; k < n; k++) {
    const a = rng() * TAU;
    const d = k === 0 ? 0 : 0.12 + rng() * 0.16 * scale;
    const mx = x + Math.cos(a) * d;
    const mz = z + Math.sin(a) * d;
    const s = scale * (k === 0 ? 1 : 0.55 + rng() * 0.4);
    writeMushroom(w, mx, mz, groundAt(mx, mz), (red ? 0.3 : 0.24) * s, (red ? 0.24 : 0.26) * s, a, cap, centre);
  }
}
```

with:

```ts
const HONEY = [new THREE.Color('#c8954a'), new THREE.Color('#6e4422')];
const RUSSULA = [new THREE.Color('#c0392b'), new THREE.Color('#7a2018')];

/**
 * A cluster of 3-5 mushrooms (honey mushrooms, or a red russula pair). `grow` scales the whole
 * cluster (the world scale mode's mushroom size, worldScale.ts); it draws no random numbers.
 */
export function writeMushrooms(
  w: MeshWriter, x: number, z: number, scale: number, rng: () => number, groundAt: GroundAt = terrainHeight,
  grow = 1,
): void {
  const red = rng() < 0.3;
  const n = red ? 2 : 3 + Math.floor(rng() * 3);
  const [cap, centre] = red ? RUSSULA : HONEY;
  for (let k = 0; k < n; k++) {
    const a = rng() * TAU;
    const d = (k === 0 ? 0 : 0.12 + rng() * 0.16 * scale) * grow;
    const mx = x + Math.cos(a) * d;
    const mz = z + Math.sin(a) * d;
    const s = scale * (k === 0 ? 1 : 0.55 + rng() * 0.4) * grow;
    writeMushroom(w, mx, mz, groundAt(mx, mz), (red ? 0.3 : 0.24) * s, (red ? 0.24 : 0.26) * s, a, cap, centre);
  }
}
```

```ts
/** A tuft of Pennsylvania sedge in fall: fine, arching golden-green blades. */
export function writeSedge(
  w: MeshWriter, x: number, z: number, scale: number, rng: () => number, groundAt: GroundAt = terrainHeight,
): void {
  const g = groundAt(x, z) - CLUTTER_SINK;
  const base = new THREE.Color('#6f7a2e');
  const tip = new THREE.Color(rng() < 0.5 ? '#c8a860' : '#b8a048');
  const blades = 9;
  for (let k = 0; k < blades; k++) {
    const yaw = (k / blades) * TAU + rng() * 0.6;
    writeBlade(w, x + Math.cos(yaw) * 0.03, g, z + Math.sin(yaw) * 0.03, yaw, (0.45 + rng() * 0.4) * scale, 0.05 * scale,
      0.45 + rng() * 0.45, 3, base, tip);
  }
}
```


Replace:

```ts
export function trunkRadius(h: number, a: number, p: TrunkParams): number {
  const hh = Math.max(0, h);
  const taper = 1 - p.taper * Math.min(1, hh / p.height);
  const collar = Math.exp(-hh / 0.4);
  const lobe = Math.max(0, Math.cos(p.lobes * a + p.phase)) ** 2;
  return p.radius * taper * (1 + p.flare * collar * (1 - p.buttress + 2 * p.buttress * lobe));
}
```

with:

```ts
export function trunkRadius(h: number, a: number, p: TrunkParams): number {
  const hh = Math.max(0, h);
  const taper = 1 - p.taper * Math.min(1, hh / p.height);
  const collar = Math.exp(-hh / (p.collar ?? 0.4));
  const lobe = Math.max(0, Math.cos(p.lobes * a + p.phase)) ** 2;
  return p.radius * taper * (1 + p.flare * collar * (1 - p.buttress + 2 * p.buttress * lobe));
}
```

### Task 8: Trunks, rocks and the log are solid for the worm

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/game/crawl.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/obstacles.ts`
- Create: `tests/unit/solidProps.test.ts`

After each step the head, then each body point, is pushed straight out of any footprint grown by the body's radius (0.13), up to three times for footprints that touch (spec 4.4). Pushing out along the normal keeps the motion along the edge, so the worm slides instead of stopping dead, and the body never cuts through. `stepWorm` stays pure. The tests drive the real crawl at every trunk, rock and the log in every mode, and check the speed test's drive never touches a prop (its head follows the free path exactly, so its numbers are unchanged).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/unit/solidProps.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect, beforeAll, afterAll } from 'vitest';
import { Crawl, BODY_RADIUS } from '../../src/game/crawl';
import { obstacleDepth, footprints } from '../../src/surface/obstacles';
import { stepWorm, type WormMotion } from '../../src/worm/locomotion';
import { AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE, LOCOMOTION } from '../../src/game/drive';
import { DEFAULT_SCALE, SCALE_MODES, setScale } from '../../src/surface/worldScale';

const DT = 1 / 60;
const hit = { nx: 0, nz: 0 };
const wrap = (a: number) => a - 2 * Math.PI * Math.round(a / (2 * Math.PI));

/** Steers toward (tx, tz) for `steps` steps; `each` sees the crawl after every step. */
function driveTo(c: Crawl, tx: number, tz: number, steps: number, each: (c: Crawl) => void): void {
  const move = { x: 0, y: 1 };
  for (let s = 0; s < steps; s++) {
    const h = c.motion.head;
    const err = wrap(Math.atan2(tx - h.x, tz - h.z) - c.motion.heading);
    move.x = Math.max(-1, Math.min(1, -err * 3));
    c.step(DT, move);
    each(c);
  }
}

/** Fails if any body point lies inside a footprint grown by the body's radius. */
function outside(c: Crawl): void {
  for (const p of c.body) expect(obstacleDepth(p.x, p.z, BODY_RADIUS, hit)).toBeLessThanOrEqual(1e-9);
}

afterAll(() => { setScale(DEFAULT_SCALE); });

describe.each(SCALE_MODES)('%s: trunks, rocks and the log are solid', (mode) => {
  beforeAll(() => { setScale(mode); });

  it('a worm driven at every one of them stops at its edge and never cuts through', () => {
    const { round, log } = footprints();
    const targets = [];
    for (let o = 0; o < round.length; o += 4) targets.push({ x: round[o], z: round[o + 1] });
    targets.push({ x: log.x, z: log.z }, { x: log.x + log.dx * log.half * 0.6, z: log.z + log.dz * log.half * 0.6 });
    for (const t of targets) {
      const c = new Crawl();
      driveTo(c, t.x, t.z, 900, outside);
    }
  }, 60_000);

  it('slides along an edge instead of stopping dead', () => {
    // Head for a point just past the log's far side, at a slant: the worm meets the log and slides.
    const { log } = footprints();
    const c = new Crawl();
    const tx = log.x - log.dz * (log.r + 3) + log.dx * 2;
    const tz = log.z + log.dx * (log.r + 3) + log.dz * 2;
    let touching = 0;
    let slid = 0;
    let last = { x: c.motion.head.x, z: c.motion.head.z };
    driveTo(c, tx, tz, 1500, (cc) => {
      const h = cc.motion.head;
      if (obstacleDepth(h.x, h.z, BODY_RADIUS + 0.01, hit) > 0) {
        touching++;
        slid += Math.hypot(h.x - last.x, h.z - last.z);
      }
      last = { x: h.x, z: h.z };
    });
    expect(touching).toBeGreaterThan(10);
    // While against the log it still moves (it steers into it, so it slides slower than it crawls).
    expect(slid / (touching * DT)).toBeGreaterThan(0.15 * LOCOMOTION.speed);
  });

  it('the speed test\'s drive never touches one: the head follows the free path exactly', () => {
    const c = new Crawl();
    let m: WormMotion = { head: { x: 0, y: 0, z: 0 }, heading: 0 };
    for (let s = 0; s < 30 * 60; s++) {
      c.step(DT, AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE.move);
      m = stepWorm(m, AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE.move, DT, LOCOMOTION);
      expect(c.motion.head.x).toBeCloseTo(m.head.x, 9);
      expect(c.motion.head.z).toBeCloseTo(m.head.z, 9);
    }
  });
});

```

In `src/surface/obstacles.ts`:

Add at the end of the file:

```ts
const slideHit: ObstacleHit = { nx: 0, nz: 0 };

/**
 * Moves the ground point `p` (x, z) straight out of any footprint grown by `radius`, the deepest
 * first, a few times over for footprints that touch: a body point pushed into a trunk, rock or
 * the log slides along its edge. Returns how far it moved in all. Allocates nothing.
 */
export function pushOut(p: { x: number; z: number }, radius: number): number {
  let moved = 0;
  for (let k = 0; k < 3; k++) {
    const into = obstacleDepth(p.x, p.z, radius, slideHit);
    if (!(into > 0)) break;
    p.x += slideHit.nx * into;
    p.z += slideHit.nz * into;
    moved += into;
  }
  return moved;
}

```

### Task 9: The camera stays out of props (rendering)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/game/crawl.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/obstacles.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/sceneTiers.test.ts`
- Modify (whole file): `tests/unit/solidProps.test.ts`

Spec 4.4: if the line from the head to where the camera wants to be crosses a footprint taller than the line there, the camera moves in along that line to stop just short. `sightReach` samples the line (48 samples, then 8 halvings) against `insideTall` (a footprint whose top, above the soil, is higher than the line: trunks always, rocks and the log only when the line passes lower than them). The crawl checks a fan of three lines (the camera's yaw ±0.5 rad), so a prop sweeping toward the middle line starts the pull-in early, and eases its reach in at 5/s and back out at 2.5/s, on top of the follow camera's own smoothing; distance and height shrink together, so the camera slides along its line. A hard limit keeps the camera itself at least 0.15 from any tall prop. Measured in the scratch build: at most 0.14 m per step with a step-to-step change of at most 0.05 (a hard turn alone moves it 0.08).

One Step 1a test changes: through a long crawl the reacting litter chunks now lie within `LOD_NEAR + LOD_GAP` of the camera rather than `LOD_NEAR` (a camera pulled in by a trunk stands nearer the head and farther from the trail); they still always draw full leaves.

```ts
/** Steers toward (tx, tz) for `steps` steps; `each` sees the crawl after every step. */

    const t = treeSites()[0];
    const foot = trunkFootOf(t);
    // From 1 m beside the trunk, looking back through it.
    const hx = t.x + foot + 1;
    const r = sightReach(hx, 0.1, t.z, hx - 4, 1.2, t.z, 0.35);
    expect(r).toBeGreaterThan(0);
    expect(r).toBeLessThan(1);
    // The stop point is the margin short of the trunk's edge.
    expect(hx - 4 * r - (t.x + foot)).toBeCloseTo(0.35, 2);
  });

  it('passes over a rock lower than the line, not one taller', () => {
    const k = rocks()[0];
    const top = terrainHeight(k.x, k.z) + k.size * 0.6;
    const foot = k.size * ROCK_FOOT;
    const hx = k.x + foot + 0.5;
    expect(sightReach(hx, top + 0.2, k.z, hx - 2 * foot - 1, top + 0.5, k.z, 0.35)).toBe(1);
    expect(sightReach(hx, top - 0.3, k.z, hx - 2 * foot - 1, top - 0.1, k.z, 0.35)).toBeLessThan(1);
  });
});

describe.each(SCALE_MODES)('%s: the camera stays out of props', (mode) => {
  beforeAll(() => { setScale(mode); });

  it('driving at and around every tall prop, the camera never enters one, and moves smoothly', () => {
    const { round, log } = footprints();
    const targets = [];
    for (let o = 0; o < round.length; o += 4) targets.push({ x: round[o], z: round[o + 1] });
    targets.push({ x: log.x + log.dx * log.half * 0.3, z: log.z + log.dz * log.half * 0.3 });
    let pulled = 0;
    let fastest = 0;
    let jerk = 0;
    for (const t of targets) {
      const c = new Crawl();
      let last = { ...c.cam.pos };
      let lastStep = { x: 0, y: 0, z: 0 };
      const check = (cc: Crawl) => {
        const p = cc.cam.pos;
        expect(insideTall(p.x, p.z, p.y)).toBe(false);
        const step = { x: p.x - last.x, y: p.y - last.y, z: p.z - last.z };
        fastest = Math.max(fastest, Math.hypot(step.x, step.y, step.z));
        jerk = Math.max(jerk, Math.hypot(step.x - lastStep.x, step.y - lastStep.y, step.z - lastStep.z));
        last = { ...p };
        lastStep = step;
        if (cc.cameraReach < 0.99) pulled++;
      };
      driveTo(c, t.x, t.z, 700, check);
      // Then turn hard on the spot against it, swinging the camera round behind.
      const move = { x: 1, y: 0.2 };
      for (let s = 0; s < 400; s++) {
        c.step(DT, move);
        check(c);
      }
    }
    // The test is real: the camera did have to pull in.
    expect(pulled).toBeGreaterThan(50);
    // It eases: at most about 10 m/s while pulling in past a trunk (its hard turns alone reach
    // about 5), and its speed changes gradually from step to step (no snap).
    expect(fastest).toBeLessThan(0.18);
    expect(jerk).toBeLessThan(0.06);
  }, 60_000);

  it('the speed test\'s drive never pulls the camera in', () => {
    const c = new Crawl();
    for (let s = 0; s < 30 * 60; s++) {
      c.step(DT, AUTO_DRIVE_MOVE.move);
      expect(c.cameraReach).toBe(1);
    }
  });
});
```

with:

```ts
      pushOut(s, BODY_RADIUS);
      s.y = this.rest(i, s.x, s.z);
    }
    this.keepCameraClear(dt);
    this.cam = stepFollowCam(this.cam, head, this.motion.heading, dt, this.camOpts);
    const c = this.cam.pos;
    // The eased target can trail a hard turn by a step or two: never let the camera itself into a
    // prop (it stops on its line to the head, a little short).
    const r = sightReach(head.x, head.y, head.z, c.x, c.y, c.z, CAM_HARD_MARGIN);
    if (r < 1) {
      c.x = head.x + (c.x - head.x) * r;
      c.y = head.y + (c.y - head.y) * r;
      c.z = head.z + (c.z - head.z) * r;
    }
    const floor = litterTop(c.x, c.z) + CAM_GROUND_MARGIN;
    if (c.y < floor) c.y = floor;
    if (this.reaction) this.disturbLeaves(dt);
    this.pushLeaves(dt);
  }

  /**
   * Keeps the camera out of tall props (spec 4.4): if the line from the head to where the camera
   * wants to be (behind it, along its yaw) runs into a trunk, rock or log taller than the line
   * there, the camera's distance and height shrink so it stops just short, easing in and back out.
   */
  private keepCameraClear(dt: number): void {
    const h = this.motion.head;
    // Three sight lines fanning out behind the head: a prop sweeping toward the middle one meets a
    // side one first, so the camera starts to ease in before it would have to jump.
    let reach = 1;
    for (let k = -1; k <= 1; k++) {
      const yaw = this.cam.yaw + k * CAM_FAN;
      reach = Math.min(reach, sightReach(
        h.x, h.y, h.z,
        h.x - Math.sin(yaw) * CAM_OPTS.distance, h.y + CAM_OPTS.height, h.z - Math.cos(yaw) * CAM_OPTS.distance,
        CAM_PROP_MARGIN,
      ));
    }
    const rate = reach < this.camReach ? CAM_IN_RATE : CAM_OUT_RATE;
    this.camReach += (reach - this.camReach) * (1 - Math.exp(-rate * dt));
    this.camOpts.distance = CAM_OPTS.distance * this.camReach;
    this.camOpts.height = CAM_OPTS.height * this.camReach;
  }

  /** Writes the render pose, the camera and `focus`, between the last two steps by `alpha`. */
```

```ts
import { treeSites, trunkFootOf } from './treeSites';
import { heroLog, rocks } from './propPlacement';

/**
```


Add at the end of the file:

```ts
/** Whether ground point (x, z) at height y lies inside a footprint that stands taller than y there. */
export function insideTall(x: number, z: number, y: number): boolean {
  const { round, log } = footprints();
  for (let o = 0; o < round.length; o += 4) {
    const dx = x - round[o];
    const dz = z - round[o + 1];
    const r = round[o + 2];
    if (dx * dx + dz * dz < r * r && terrainHeight(round[o], round[o + 1]) + round[o + 3] > y) return true;
  }
  const t = Math.max(-log.half, Math.min(log.half, (x - log.x) * log.dx + (z - log.z) * log.dz));
  const cx = log.x + log.dx * t;
  const cz = log.z + log.dz * t;
  const dx = x - cx;
  const dz = z - cz;
  return dx * dx + dz * dz < log.r * log.r && terrainHeight(cx, cz) + log.top > y;
}

/** Samples along the sight line, then halvings of the first blocked stretch. */
const SIGHT_SAMPLES = 48;
const SIGHT_HALVINGS = 8;

/**
 * How far along the line from (hx, hy, hz) to (tx, ty, tz) the camera can go (0..1) without
 * entering a solid prop taller than the line there, stopping `margin` (m) short of it: 1 when the
 * line is clear. A rock or the log lower than the line is passed over. Allocates nothing.
 */
export function sightReach(
  hx: number, hy: number, hz: number, tx: number, ty: number, tz: number, margin: number,
): number {
  const len = Math.hypot(tx - hx, tz - hz);
  if (!(len > 1e-6)) return 1;
  let clear = 0;
  for (let k = 1; k <= SIGHT_SAMPLES; k++) {
    const t = k / SIGHT_SAMPLES;
    if (!insideTall(hx + (tx - hx) * t, hz + (tz - hz) * t, hy + (ty - hy) * t)) {
      clear = t;
      continue;
    }
    let lo = clear;
    let hi = t;
    for (let h = 0; h < SIGHT_HALVINGS; h++) {
      const mid = (lo + hi) / 2;
      if (insideTall(hx + (tx - hx) * mid, hz + (tz - hz) * mid, hy + (ty - hy) * mid)) hi = mid;
      else lo = mid;
    }
    return Math.max(0, lo - margin / len);
  }
  return 1;
}
```

### Task 10: The disturbance map sized for True's leaves (rendering)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/game/crawl.ts`
- Modify: `src/render/leafGlsl.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/disturbance.ts`
- Modify (whole file): `src/surface/leafScale.ts`
- Modify: `tests/e2e/glslParity.spec.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/disturbance.test.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/leafGlsl.test.ts`

Spec 4.3: no shader recompiles from switching, so the map is sized once for the biggest leaves. True's biggest litter leaf reaches 3.06 from its centre (`maxLeafRadius`: Classic's 1.26 times the largest True factor). The map's reach becomes 3.65 (that leaf, plus the drape's foot 0.34, plus room for the 0.2 m a body riding on top still reaches: `ON_TOP_DISTANCE = DIST_REACH - 0.2`). Its span grows to 12 m on 96 × 96 cells, keeping 12.5 cm cells, so a True leaf touching the head still sits inside the shader's unfaded middle.

To keep Classic's cost, each mode stamps only as far as its own leaves need (`stampLimit`: biggest leaf plus the foot; Classic 1.6 m, exactly Step 1a's reach). An empty cell reads as "far", which is what a far cell would read anyway. Measured on the dev Mac: Classic stamping unchanged; True 0.05 → 0.22 ms per step (Task 16 checks the cost on the gates).

The lid rule (leafGlsl): a leaf whose radius is over 1.3 lifts level instead of tilting onto the body, blending in up to 2.0. Only True's leaves are that big; across a leaf that size the worm can curve away under the low side of a tilted plane (the clearance test found 9 vertices within 5 mm). Classic and Hybrid leaves are unaffected. GLSL and the TS twin change together; `glslParity` now reads the map's size from `DIST_N` instead of a hard-coded 64.

```ts
}

/** Stamps one body point at (x, z): a segment of no length (see stampSegment). */
export function stamp(m: DisturbanceMap, x: number, z: number, under: number): void {
  stampSegment(m, x, z, x, z, under, under);
}

/**
 * Stamps the whole body: the segments joining consecutive body points (x, z of each, in order),
 * each end as far under the leaves as `under` says for that point.
 */
export function stampBody(m: DisturbanceMap, body: readonly Vec3[], under: ArrayLike<number>): void {
  for (let i = 0; i + 1 < body.length; i++) {
    stampSegment(m, body[i].x, body[i].z, body[i + 1].x, body[i + 1].z, under[i], under[i + 1]);
  }
}
```

with:

```ts
}

/** Stamps one body point at (x, z): a segment of no length (see stampSegment). */
export function stamp(m: DisturbanceMap, x: number, z: number, under: number, limit = DIST_REACH): void {
  stampSegment(m, x, z, x, z, under, under, limit);
}

/**
 * Stamps the whole body: the segments joining consecutive body points (x, z of each, in order),
 * each end as far under the leaves as `under` says for that point. Cells `limit` or more from the
 * body stay empty: a mode with smaller leaves passes how far they need it (stampLimit), which saves
 * most of the work, and those leaves read an empty cell exactly as they would read a far one.
 */
export function stampBody(m: DisturbanceMap, body: readonly Vec3[], under: ArrayLike<number>, limit = DIST_REACH): void {
  for (let i = 0; i + 1 < body.length; i++) {
    stampSegment(m, body[i].x, body[i].z, body[i + 1].x, body[i + 1].z, under[i], under[i + 1], limit);
  }
}
```

Replace the whole of `src/surface/leafScale.ts` with:

```ts
import { LEAF_KINDS, type LeafKind } from './leafLitter';
import { leafShape } from './leafShapes';
import { LEAF_SIZE } from './leafPalette';
import { scaleSpec, type ScaleSpec } from './worldScale';
import { DIST_REACH } from './disturbance';

/**
 * The farthest any Classic litter leaf reaches from its centre (the largest placement scale of the
 * biggest species, petiole included; tests/unit/leafGlsl.test.ts checks it). Detail leaves are
 * smaller, and hero leaves do not read the disturbance map.
 */
export const CLASSIC_LEAF_RADIUS = 1.26;
/** The drape's foot (leafGlsl.ts DRAPE_FOOT; tests/unit/disturbance.test.ts checks they agree). */
const DRAPE_FOOT_M = 0.34;

/** A species' silhouette area at length 1 (its outline, leaf units). */
function shapeArea(kind: LeafKind): number {
  const s = leafShape(kind);
  let a = 0;
  for (let j = 0; j < s.outline; j++) {
    const k = (j + 1) % s.outline;
    a += s.points[j * 2] * s.points[k * 2 + 1] - s.points[k * 2] * s.points[j * 2 + 1];
  }
  return Math.abs(a) / 2;
}
const AREA = Object.fromEntries(LEAF_KINDS.map((k) => [k, shapeArea(k)])) as Record<LeafKind, number>;

/**
 * Bigger leaves, fewer of them (spec 4.2): a leaf layer's count scales with 1 / leaf area, so the
 * floor stays about as covered (randomly strewn leaves cover a share that depends only on their
 * total area) and overdraw and fill rate stay about the same. Species are drawn equally often, so
 * the area is summed over them, each with its own silhouette. 1 for Classic and Hybrid.
 */
export function leafCountScale(spec: ScaleSpec = scaleSpec()): number {
  let base = 0;
  let scaled = 0;
  for (const k of LEAF_KINDS) {
    base += AREA[k] * LEAF_SIZE[k] ** 2;
    scaled += AREA[k] * (LEAF_SIZE[k] * spec.leaf[k]) ** 2;
  }
  return base / scaled;
}

/** A preset's leaf count (graphics.ts gives Classic's) for the mode: at least 1 when n > 0. */
export function scaledLeafCount(n: number, spec: ScaleSpec = scaleSpec()): number {
  return n > 0 ? Math.max(1, Math.round(n * leafCountScale(spec))) : 0;
}

/** The farthest a litter leaf of the mode reaches from its centre. */
export function maxLeafRadius(spec: ScaleSpec = scaleSpec()): number {
  return CLASSIC_LEAF_RADIUS * Math.max(...LEAF_KINDS.map((k) => spec.leaf[k]));
}

/**
 * How far from the body the mode's leaves need the disturbance map stamped (stampBody's limit):
 * the biggest leaf plus the drape's foot (Classic: 1.6 m, the map's reach before Part A). Never
 * past the map's reach.
 */
export function stampLimit(spec: ScaleSpec = scaleSpec()): number {
  return Math.min(DIST_REACH, maxLeafRadius(spec) + DRAPE_FOOT_M);
}
```

### Task 11: Leaves at true size, and every clearance test in every mode (rendering)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/game/world.ts`
- Modify: `src/main.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/detailLeaves.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/heroLeaves.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/heroPool.ts`
- Modify: `src/surface/leafLitterMesh.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/heroLeafClearance.test.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/leafGlsl.test.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/leafJitter.test.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/nearClutter.test.ts`

Spec 4.5. Litter, detail and hero leaves multiply their size by the species' factor (`spec.leaf[kind]`: 1 in Classic and Hybrid). The litter is then real length on average; detail and hero leaves keep their Classic share of it. Counts follow 1 / area (`scaledLeafCount`) for the world's build, the preset's draw counts and the hero count. Hero leaf sizes are set by `sizeHeroLeaves`, which Task 12 calls again after a switch.

Hero leaves longer than 1 m (only True's) tilt and settle at rates divided by their length cubed: without it True's hero leaves turned back by more than 2 mm on 4.0% of frames (threshold 3%). With it: 2.8% and a p99 of 5.3 mm (threshold 6 mm). Classic's rates are unchanged.

The drape (litter and detail), hero-leaf and near-clutter clearance tests now run in every mode, and the Part 0 jitter tests run in Classic and True with the same thresholds. True's litter test samples every vertex (it has a quarter as many leaves); the hero leaves' median gap bound is 0.15 m in True (bigger leaves span more of the body; measured 0.13) and stays 0.12 elsewhere.

```ts
      continue;
```

```ts
/**
 * Sets every slot's leaf length from its fixed base (0.55 to 1 m) times its species' size in the
 * active scale mode (worldScale.ts). createHeroLeaves calls it; call it again after a mode change.
 */
export function sizeHeroLeaves(p: HeroLeaves): void {
  const grow = scaleSpec().leaf;
  for (let i = 0; i < p.max; i++) p.size[i] = (0.55 + 0.45 * hash01(i, p.seed + 101)) * grow[LEAF_KINDS[p.kind[i]]];
```

### Task 12: Rebuild the forest on a switch without recompiling (rendering)

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/game/crawl.ts`
- Modify (whole file): `src/game/world.ts`
- Modify: `src/render/groundTexture.ts`
- Modify: `src/render/litterTexture.ts`
- Create: `tests/unit/worldRebuild.test.ts`

Spec 4.3: changing mode rebuilds the forest behind the Start screen. `buildWorld` now keeps the scale-dependent parts in one `Forest` group: the ground, near trees and log, distant ring, litter, detail layer and ground props. `rebuildForest()` re-bakes the litter and ground textures **in place** (same texture objects, so every material keeps its uniform), builds the new forest, swaps it into the scene and returns the old one. The caller then calls `apply()`, `compileForest()` (which compiles the new materials, lending the litter's reacting material to a chunk so it compiles too) and only then `disposeForest(old)`. Because the old materials still hold their programs while the new, identical ones compile, three.js reuses every program: Task 13's e2e test counts zero `compileShader` calls across switches. The sky, atmosphere, hero leaves, near clutter and worm stay. `Crawl.applyScale()` re-sizes the hero leaves and the stamp limit; `reset()` then puts the worm at the spawn.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/unit/worldRebuild.test.ts`:

```ts
import { describe, it, expect, afterAll } from 'vitest';
import * as THREE from 'three';
import { buildWorld, disposeForest } from '../../src/game/world';
import { Crawl } from '../../src/game/crawl';
import { PRESETS } from '../../src/render/graphics';
import { getLitterTexture, litterTextureData } from '../../src/render/litterTexture';
import { getGroundTexture, groundTextureData } from '../../src/render/groundTexture';
import { scaledLeafCount, stampLimit } from '../../src/surface/leafScale';
import { DEFAULT_SCALE, SCALE_SPECS, setScale } from '../../src/surface/worldScale';
import { LEAF_KINDS } from '../../src/surface/leafLitter';

afterAll(() => { setScale(DEFAULT_SCALE); });

describe('switching the world scale rebuilds the forest in place', () => {
  it('swaps the forest, keeps the rest, and the old one can be freed', () => {
    setScale('classic');
    const crawl = new Crawl();
    const world = buildWorld(crawl);
    world.apply(PRESETS.high);
    const keep = [world.worm.group, world.hero.mesh, world.nearClutter.group, world.atmosphere.group];
    const litterTex = getLitterTexture();
    const groundTex = getGroundTexture();
    const before = world.litter;
    expect(before.max).toBe(PRESETS.ultra.litter);

    setScale('true');
    const old = world.rebuildForest();
    world.apply(PRESETS.high);
    expect(old.litter).toBe(before);
    expect(world.scene.children).not.toContain(old.group);
    expect(world.scene.children.filter((c) => c.name === 'forest')).toHaveLength(1);
    for (const o of keep) expect(world.scene.children).toContain(o);
    // The new forest is the mode's: fewer, bigger leaves; the detail layer was showing, so it is built.
    expect(world.litter).not.toBe(before);
    expect(world.litter.max).toBe(scaledLeafCount(PRESETS.ultra.litter, SCALE_SPECS.true));
    expect(world.litter.drawn).toBe(scaledLeafCount(PRESETS.high.litter, SCALE_SPECS.true));
    expect(world.detail.built).not.toBeNull();
    expect(world.detail.drawn).toBe(scaledLeafCount(PRESETS.high.detail, SCALE_SPECS.true));
    // The baked textures are the same objects, re-baked for True.
    expect(getLitterTexture()).toBe(litterTex);
    expect(getGroundTexture()).toBe(groundTex);
    expect(Array.from(litterTex.image.data as Uint8Array)).toEqual(Array.from(litterTextureData()));
    expect(Array.from(groundTex.image.data as Float32Array)).toEqual(Array.from(groundTextureData()));

    // Freeing the old forest disposes every geometry and material it drew with.
    let disposed = 0;
    const meshes: THREE.Mesh[] = [];
    old.group.traverse((o) => { if (o instanceof THREE.Mesh) meshes.push(o); });
    const mats = new Set<THREE.Material>([old.litter.materials.active, old.litter.materials.plain, old.litter.materials.far]);
    for (const m of meshes) {
      m.geometry.addEventListener('dispose', () => { disposed++; });
      mats.add(m.material as THREE.Material);
    }
    for (const m of mats) m.addEventListener('dispose', () => { disposed++; });
    disposeForest(old);
    expect(disposed).toBe(meshes.length + mats.size);
  });

  it('the crawl takes the mode\'s hero leaf sizes and stamp reach', () => {
    setScale('classic');
    const crawl = new Crawl();
    const classic = Array.from(crawl.hero.size);
    setScale('true');
    crawl.applyScale();
    crawl.reset();
    for (let i = 0; i < classic.length; i++) {
      const kind = LEAF_KINDS[crawl.hero.kind[i]];
      expect(crawl.hero.size[i]).toBeCloseTo(classic[i] * SCALE_SPECS.true.leaf[kind], 5);
    }
    expect(stampLimit()).toBeGreaterThan(stampLimit(SCALE_SPECS.classic));
    expect(crawl.motion.head).toMatchObject({ x: 0, z: 0 });
  });
});
```

Replace the whole of `src/game/world.ts` with:

```ts
import * as THREE from 'three';
import { buildSky } from '../render/sky';
import { buildAtmosphere, type Atmosphere } from '../render/atmosphere';
import { setRimLight } from '../render/paintedMaterial';
import {
  groundUniforms, setFocus, setLeafReaction, setSpine, uploadDisturbance,
} from '../render/groundUniforms';
import { occluderSegments } from '../render/occluderSegments';
import { refreshLitterTexture } from '../render/litterTexture';
import { refreshGroundTexture } from '../render/groundTexture';
import { disturbedBox } from '../surface/disturbance';
import { PRESETS, type PresetSpec } from '../render/graphics';
import { buildTerrain } from '../surface/terrainMesh';
import { buildForestTrees } from '../surface/forestTrees';
import { buildDistantTrees } from '../surface/distantTrees';
import { buildLeafLitter, type LeafLitter } from '../surface/leafLitterMesh';
import { buildDetailLayer, type DetailLayer } from '../surface/detailLeaves';
import { HeroLeafMesh } from '../surface/heroLeafMesh';
import { buildNearClutterLayer, type NearClutterLayer } from '../surface/nearClutter';
import { buildGroundProps, writeHeroLog, type GroundProps } from '../surface/groundProps';
import { scaledLeafCount } from '../surface/leafScale';
import { HeroWorm, type WormPose } from '../worm/wormMesh';
import { wormSurface, type WormSurface } from '../worm/surfaceHeight';
import { WORM_SEGMENTS, type Crawl } from './crawl';

/**
 * Everything the world scale mode sizes and places (worldScale.ts): the ground, the near and
 * distant trees with the log, the litter, the detail leaves and the ground props. Rebuilt when the
 * mode changes; the rest of the world stays.
 */
export interface Forest {
  readonly group: THREE.Group;
  readonly litter: LeafLitter;
  readonly detail: DetailLayer;
  readonly props: GroundProps;
}

export interface World {
  scene: THREE.Scene;
  /** The base litter in chunks (full and low-detail meshes), built with Ultra's leaf count. */
  readonly litter: LeafLitter;
  /** The close-up leaf layer around the worm (built when a preset first draws it). */
  readonly detail: DetailLayer;
  /** The loose physics leaves near the head (one instanced draw; the crawl's pool sets the count). */
  hero: HeroLeafMesh;
  /** Rocks and ground clutter. */
  readonly props: GroundProps;
  /** Ultra's extra clutter around the worm (built when Ultra is first shown). */
  nearClutter: NearClutterLayer;
  /** Light shafts, dust motes and falling leaves. */
  atmosphere: Atmosphere;
  /** Continuous tube body plus its contact shadow. */
  worm: HeroWorm;
  /**
   * Shows a preset: counts by draw range, effects by visibility, rim light by uniform. Allocates
   * nothing. The renderer's pixel ratio is the caller's.
   */
  apply(spec: PresetSpec): void;
  /** Poses everything that moves with the simulation for this rendered frame. Allocates nothing. */
  sync(crawl: Crawl, alpha: number, timeSec: number, camera: THREE.PerspectiveCamera): void;
  /**
   * After setScale: builds the forest for the new mode in place of the old one (the baked litter
   * and ground textures re-baked in place) and returns the old one. Call apply() again, then
   * compileForest() so the new materials take over the old ones' compiled programs, and only then
   * dispose the old forest (disposeForest): nothing recompiles.
   */
  rebuildForest(): Forest;
  /** Compiles every program the forest can draw with (the litter's reacting one too), now. */
  compileForest(renderer: THREE.WebGLRenderer, camera: THREE.Camera): void;
}

/** The scale mode's forest, built at Ultra size so presets only trim it. */
function buildForest(): Forest {
  const group = new THREE.Group();
  group.name = 'forest';
  // Built at Ultra's counts for the scale mode (bigger leaves, fewer of them: leafScale.ts).
  const litter = buildLeafLitter(scaledLeafCount(PRESETS.ultra.litter));
  const detail = buildDetailLayer(scaledLeafCount(PRESETS.ultra.detail));
  const props = buildGroundProps();
  // The ground fills most of the screen: draw it after everything that sits on it, so the
  // depth test skips ground pixels already covered by leaves, props and trunks.
  const terrain = buildTerrain();
  terrain.renderOrder = 5;
  group.add(terrain, buildForestTrees(writeHeroLog), buildDistantTrees(), litter.group, detail.group, props.mesh);
  return { group, litter, detail, props };
}

/** Frees a forest's geometries and materials (after its replacement has compiled). */
export function disposeForest(f: Forest): void {
  const materials = new Set<THREE.Material>([f.litter.materials.active, f.litter.materials.plain, f.litter.materials.far]);
  f.group.traverse((o) => {
    if (!(o instanceof THREE.Mesh)) return;
    o.geometry.dispose();
    for (const m of Array.isArray(o.material) ? o.material : [o.material]) materials.add(m);
  });
  for (const m of materials) m.dispose();
}

/**
 * The forest floor, built once at Ultra size so presets only trim it. Thin composer: every
 * part is built in its own module.
 */
export function buildWorld(crawl: Crawl): World {
  const scene = new THREE.Scene();
  let forest = buildForest();
  const hero = new HeroLeafMesh(crawl.hero);
  const nearClutter = buildNearClutterLayer();
  const atmosphere = buildAtmosphere();
  // The hero worm: no eyes (spec 8.3); it is posed from the simulated spine every frame.
  let reaction = true;
  const surf: WormSurface = { y: 0, under: 0 };
  // The contact shadow lies on the same surface the worm rests on.
  const worm = new HeroWorm(WORM_SEGMENTS, (x, z) => wormSurface(x, z, reaction, surf).y);
  const pose: WormPose = { spine: new Float32Array(WORM_SEGMENTS * 3), phase: 0, activity: 0, timeSec: 0 };
  const occBox = new Float32Array(5);
  // Where the uploaded map can move a leaf (disturbedBox); empty until the first upload.
  const distBox = new Float32Array([Infinity, Infinity, -Infinity, -Infinity]);

  scene.add(forest.group, hero.mesh, nearClutter.group, worm.group, buildSky(), atmosphere.group);
  return {
    scene, hero, nearClutter, atmosphere, worm,
    get litter() { return forest.litter; },
    get detail() { return forest.detail; },
    get props() { return forest.props; },
    apply(spec) {
      reaction = spec.reaction;
      forest.litter.setCount(scaledLeafCount(spec.litter));
      forest.detail.setCount(scaledLeafCount(spec.detail));
      forest.props.setDensity(spec.clutter);
      nearClutter.show(spec.nearClutter);
      atmosphere.setLevel(spec.effects, spec.extraEffects);
      setRimLight(spec.rim);
      setLeafReaction(spec.reaction);
    },
    rebuildForest() {
      const old = forest;
      refreshLitterTexture();
      refreshGroundTexture();
      forest = buildForest();
      // A detail layer that was built is built again at once (apply() sets its count), so it
      // compiles with the rest.
      if (old.detail.built) forest.detail.setCount(forest.detail.max);
      scene.remove(old.group);
      scene.add(forest.group);
      return old;
    },
    compileForest(renderer, camera) {
      // The litter's reacting program is only put on a chunk when the worm comes near: lend it to
      // one for the compile, so it is ready too.
      const near = forest.litter.chunks[0].near;
      const was = near.material;
      near.material = forest.litter.materials.active;
      try {
        renderer.compile(forest.group, camera, scene);
      } finally {
        near.material = was;
      }
    },
    sync(crawl, alpha, timeSec, camera) {
      crawl.interpolate(alpha, timeSec, pose, camera);
      worm.update(pose);
      hero.update(crawl.hero, alpha);
      setFocus(crawl.focus.x, crawl.focus.y, crawl.focus.z);
      if (nearClutter.visible) setSpine(pose.spine);
      // The leaf fade (every preset): the body as this frame's camera sees it.
      camera.updateMatrixWorld();
      const pe = camera.projectionMatrix.elements;
      const g = groundUniforms;
      const view = camera.matrixWorldInverse.elements;
      g.uOccCount.value = occluderSegments(pose.spine, view, pe[0], pe[5], g.uOccSeg.value, occBox);
      g.uOccBox.value.fromArray(occBox);
      g.uOccDepth.value = occBox[4];
      g.uOccTan.value.set(1 / pe[0], 1 / pe[5]);
      if (crawl.disturbed) {
        uploadDisturbance(crawl.disturbance);
        disturbedBox(crawl.disturbance, distBox);
        crawl.disturbed = false;
      }
      // Per litter chunk: full or low-detail leaves, and whether it may react (as uploaded now).
      forest.litter.update(camera.position.x, camera.position.z, pose.spine, distBox);
    },
  };
}
```

### Task 13: The World scale screen

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/game/screens.ts`
- Modify: `src/main.ts`
- Modify: `src/perf/speedTest.ts`
- Modify: `src/perf/speedTestRunner.ts`
- Modify: `src/ui/icons.ts`
- Modify: `src/ui/menus.ts`
- Modify: `src/ui/parchment.css`
- Create: `src/ui/scaleMenu.ts`
- Modify: `src/ui/shell.ts`
- Create: `tests/e2e/scale.spec.ts`
- Modify: `tests/e2e/shell.spec.ts`

- Modify: `tests/unit/screens.test.ts`
- Modify: `tests/unit/speedTest.test.ts`

Start gains **World scale** (after Crawl; spec 4.3). Its card has three real radio buttons with a name and one line each, a status line inside the card's `aria-live` region ("Building the forest…", then "The forest is ready: True.") and Back. It follows the menu rules: real DOM, 18 px or more, the parchment palette (7:1), a 4 px focus ring (`.choice:focus-within`), 64 px targets, focus on the checked choice, Esc and Back return focus to the World scale button.

A pick is saved at once (`updateSettings`), and built 400 ms after the last pick, so arrowing from Classic to True builds once. Leaving the screen builds a waiting pick at once. `main.ts` sets the scale before building anything (`?scale=` first, then the saved choice) and `switchScale` follows Task 12's order. The speed-test report names the mode ("World scale: True").

Existing e2e tests that tab from Crawl to Graphics or Speed test now pass World scale on the way.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests**

Create `tests/e2e/scale.spec.ts`:

```ts
import { test, expect, type Page } from '@playwright/test';
import path from 'node:path';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';
import { DEFAULT_SCALE, SCALE_LABELS, SCALE_MODES, type ScaleMode } from '../../src/surface/worldScale';

const PAGE = pathToFileURL(path.resolve('dist/index.html')).href;

function watchErrors(page: Page): string[] {
  const errors: string[] = [];
  page.on('pageerror', (e) => errors.push(e.message));
  page.on('console', (m) => {
    if (m.type() === 'error' && !m.text().includes('GPU stall')) errors.push(m.text());
  });
  return errors;
}

const button = (page: Page, name: string) => page.getByRole('button', { name, exact: true });
const radio = (page: Page, mode: ScaleMode) => page.getByRole('radio', { name: new RegExp(`^${SCALE_LABELS[mode]}`) });
const saved = (page: Page) => page.evaluate(() => JSON.parse(localStorage.getItem('wormsim.settings') ?? 'null'));
const ready = (page: Page, mode: ScaleMode) =>
  expect(page.getByText(`The forest is ready: ${SCALE_LABELS[mode]}.`)).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30_000 });

test('keyboard only: Start -> World scale, pick a mode, it builds, saves, and Esc returns focus', async ({ page }) => {
  const errors = watchErrors(page);
  await page.goto(PAGE);
  await expect(button(page, 'Crawl')).toBeFocused();
  await page.keyboard.press('Tab');
  await expect(button(page, 'World scale')).toBeFocused();
  await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
  await expect(page.getByRole('region', { name: 'World scale' })).toBeVisible();
  await expect(radio(page, DEFAULT_SCALE)).toBeChecked();
  await expect(radio(page, DEFAULT_SCALE)).toBeFocused();
  for (const m of SCALE_MODES) await expect(page.getByText(SCALE_LABELS[m], { exact: true })).toBeVisible();

  // Arrow down to the next mode (it wraps round): it says it is building, then that it is ready.
  const next = SCALE_MODES[(SCALE_MODES.indexOf(DEFAULT_SCALE) + 1) % SCALE_MODES.length];
  await page.keyboard.press('ArrowDown');
  await expect(radio(page, next)).toBeChecked();
  await expect(page.getByText('Building the forest…')).toBeVisible();
  await ready(page, next);
  await expect(radio(page, next)).toBeFocused();
  expect((await saved(page)).scale).toBe(next);

  await page.keyboard.press('Escape');
  await expect(button(page, 'World scale')).toBeFocused();
  // It is still that mode after a reload.
  await page.reload();
  await button(page, 'World scale').click();
  await expect(radio(page, next)).toBeChecked();
  expect(errors).toEqual([]);
});

test('switching modes rebuilds the forest without compiling a single shader, offline, with no errors', async ({ page, context }) => {
  const errors = watchErrors(page);
  const outside: string[] = [];
  page.on('request', (r) => { if (!/^(file|data|blob):/.test(r.url())) outside.push(r.url()); });
  await page.addInitScript(() => {
    const w = window as unknown as { __compiles: number };
    w.__compiles = 0;
    const proto = WebGL2RenderingContext.prototype;
    const compile = proto.compileShader;
    proto.compileShader = function (s) { w.__compiles++; return compile.call(this, s); };
  });
  await context.setOffline(true);
  await page.goto(PAGE + '?scale=classic');
  // Let the first frames compile everything (the litter's reacting program on its first frame).
  await page.waitForTimeout(2000);
  const compiles = () => page.evaluate(() => (window as unknown as { __compiles: number }).__compiles);
  const before = await compiles();
  expect(before).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  await button(page, 'World scale').click();
  for (const m of ['true', 'hybrid', 'classic', 'true'] as ScaleMode[]) {
    await radio(page, m).check();
    await ready(page, m);
    await page.waitForTimeout(300);
  }
  expect(await compiles()).toBe(before);
  // And the crawl runs in the new forest.
  await button(page, 'Back').click();
  await button(page, 'Crawl').click();
  await page.keyboard.down('ArrowUp');
  await page.waitForTimeout(600);
  await page.keyboard.up('ArrowUp');
  expect(await compiles()).toBe(before);
  expect(outside).toEqual([]);
  expect(errors).toEqual([]);
});

test('?scale= forces a mode for the visit without changing the saved one', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto(PAGE);
  await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.setItem('wormsim.settings', JSON.stringify({ version: 1, graphics: 'auto', autoSettled: null, scale: 'hybrid' })));
  await page.goto(PAGE + '?scale=true');
  await button(page, 'World scale').click();
  await expect(radio(page, 'true')).toBeChecked();
  expect((await saved(page)).scale).toBe('hybrid');
});

test('a Step 1a save (no scale) loads at the default scale and keeps its graphics', async ({ page }) => {
  const errors = watchErrors(page);
  await page.goto(PAGE);
  await page.evaluate(() => localStorage.setItem('wormsim.settings', JSON.stringify({ version: 1, graphics: 'low', autoSettled: null })));
  await page.reload();
  await button(page, 'World scale').click();
  await expect(radio(page, DEFAULT_SCALE)).toBeChecked();
  await button(page, 'Back').click();
  await button(page, 'Graphics').click();
  await expect(page.getByRole('radio', { name: /^Low/ })).toBeChecked();
  expect(errors).toEqual([]);
});

test('the speed test report names the world scale', async ({ page }) => {
  await page.goto(PAGE + '?quick=1&speedtest=1&scale=true');
  await button(page, 'Start speed test').click();
  await expect(page.getByTestId('result')).toContainText('World scale: True', { timeout: 30_000 });
});
```

with:

```ts
    }

  /**
   * Rebuilds the forest for `mode` behind the Start screen (Step 1b spec 4.3): the new forest takes
   * over the old one's compiled shaders before the old one is freed, so nothing recompiles; the
   * crawl starts again at the spawn.
   */
  function switchScale(mode: ScaleMode): void {
    if (mode === scaleSpec().mode) return;
    setScale(mode);
    crawl.applyScale();
    crawl.reset();
    const old = world.rebuildForest();
    applySpec(spec);
    world.compileForest(renderer, camera);
    disposeForest(old);
  }

  /** A World scale pick: saved now, built once the picking settles (SCALE_SETTLE_MS). */
  function chooseScale(mode: ScaleMode): void {
    updateSettings(store, { scale: mode });
    pendingScale = mode;
    if (scaleTimer !== null) clearTimeout(scaleTimer);
    const was = scaleStatus;
    scaleStatus = 'building';
    if (was !== 'building') draw();
    scaleTimer = setTimeout(finishScale, SCALE_SETTLE_MS);
  }

  /** Builds a waiting pick now (also when the player leaves the screen first). */
  function finishScale(): void {
    if (pendingScale === null) return;
    if (scaleTimer !== null) clearTimeout(scaleTimer);
    const mode = pendingScale;
    pendingScale = null;
    scaleTimer = null;
    switchScale(mode);
    scaleStatus = 'ready';
    if (state.screen === 'scale') draw();
  }

  function resize(): void {
    renderer.setSize(window.innerWidth, window.innerHeight);
    camera.aspect = window.innerWidth / window.innerHeight;
```

```ts
import type { Shell } from './shell';
import { VERSION_LABEL } from '../version';

export interface StartActions { onCrawl(): void; onScale(): void; onGraphics(): void; onSpeedTest(): void }

/**
 * Start: the parchment title card over the live forest. `from`: the screen we came back from, so
 * focus returns to the button that opened it (Crawl otherwise).
 */
export function showStart(shell: Shell, a: StartActions, from: 'graphics' | 'scale' | null = null): void {
  const crawl = button('Crawl', 'play', 'primary', a.onCrawl);
  const scale = button('World scale', 'ruler', 'secondary', a.onScale);
  const graphics = button('Graphics', 'sliders', 'secondary', a.onGraphics);
  shell.show('start', 'Start', [
    cardHead('A forest floor in fall'),
    el('p', 'You are an earthworm under the leaves of a northern forest.', 'lead'),
    menu(crawl, scale, graphics, button('Speed test', 'gauge', 'secondary', a.onSpeedTest)),
    el('p', VERSION_LABEL, 'version'),
  ]);
  (from === 'graphics' ? graphics : from === 'scale' ? scale : crawl).el.focus();
}

/** Crawl: a small hint card; focus rests on it so Space and arrows press no button. */
```

Create `src/ui/scaleMenu.ts`:

```ts
import { el, button, menu } from './dom';
import { SCALE_MODES, SCALE_LABELS, SCALE_DESCRIPTIONS, type ScaleMode } from '../surface/worldScale';
import type { Shell } from './shell';

/** What the World scale screen says under its choices (the card is the aria-live region). */
export type ScaleStatus = 'idle' | 'building' | 'ready';

export const BUILDING_TEXT = 'Building the forest…';

/**
 * The World scale screen (Step 1b spec 4.3): three real radio buttons (Classic, Hybrid, True), each
 * with its one line, and Back. A pick is the caller's to save and build; `status` says what the
 * forest is doing. Focus rests on the checked choice (or Back), so redrawing keeps the player's place.
 */
export function showScale(
  shell: Shell, current: ScaleMode, status: ScaleStatus,
  onChoose: (m: ScaleMode) => void, onBack: () => void,
): void {
  const set = el('fieldset', '', 'choices');
  set.append(el('legend', 'World scale', 'screen-title'));
  set.append(el('p', 'How big is the forest next to the worm?', 'lead'));
  let checked: HTMLInputElement | null = null;
  for (const m of SCALE_MODES) {
    const id = `scale-${m}`;
    const input = el('input');
    input.type = 'radio';
    input.name = 'scale';
    input.id = id;
    input.value = m;
    input.checked = m === current;
    input.setAttribute('aria-labelledby', `${id}-name`);
    input.setAttribute('aria-describedby', `${id}-desc`);
    input.addEventListener('change', () => { if (input.checked) onChoose(m); });
    if (input.checked) checked = input;
    const label = el('label', '', 'choice');
    label.htmlFor = id;
    const words = el('span', '', 'choice-words');
    const desc = el('span', SCALE_DESCRIPTIONS[m], 'choice-desc');
    desc.id = `${id}-desc`;
    const name = el('span', SCALE_LABELS[m], 'choice-name');
    name.id = `${id}-name`;
    words.append(name, desc);
    label.append(input, words);
    set.append(label);
  }
  const note = el('p', status === 'building' ? BUILDING_TEXT
    : status === 'ready' ? `The forest is ready: ${SCALE_LABELS[current]}.` : '', 'scale-status');
  const back = button('Back', 'back', 'secondary', onBack);
  shell.show('scale', 'World scale', [set, note, menu(back)]);
  (checked ?? back.el).focus();
}
```

### Task 14: Performance gates run True and Classic

**Files:**
- Modify (whole file): `scripts/perf-gates.mjs`

Spec 4.6: `node scripts/perf-gates.mjs 4` now runs every preset in True and in Classic (`--scale=true,classic` by default; `--scale=hybrid` for one mode), interleaved with the Step 0 page as before, labels each line `scale/preset`, and fails a line whose draw calls exceed 40. `--list` prints the run order and exits, so the argument handling can be checked without benchmarking. Do **not** run the gates in this task (Task 16 does, with no other agent running).

- [ ] **Step 1: Implement**

Replace the whole of `scripts/perf-gates.mjs` with:

```js
// Performance gates (spec 2026-10-01 section 6; Step 1b spec 4.6): each preset's uncapped
// speed-test fps must be at least a fixed fraction of the Step 0 page's fps, measured back to back
// on the same machine so background load cancels out. Every gate holds in every world scale run.
//
// Usage: npm run build && node scripts/perf-gates.mjs [cycles=4] [--scale=true,classic] [--list] [presets...]
// It builds the Step 0 baseline once into .cache/step0 (git worktree at STEP0_REF), then runs
// S0, p1, p2, S0, p3, p4, S0 ... (each preset in each scale) and prints each one's median ratio
// against its gate. By default it runs True (the default mode) and Classic. --list prints the run
// order and exits. Takes ~4 min per cycle per scale. Close other heavy apps first; results are
// still noisy (±5%).
import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import path from 'node:path';

const STEP0_REF = '966b874'; // the final Step 0 page on main, the baseline Step 1a's gates were measured against
// The original absolute gates (50/40/34/28 fps) were set against the Step 0 prototype's 45.9 fps on a
// quiet machine; the final Step 0 page runs within ~5% of that, so the ratios carry over.
const STEP0_FPS = 45.9;
const GATES = { low: 50 / STEP0_FPS, medium: 40 / STEP0_FPS, high: 34 / STEP0_FPS, ultra: 28 / STEP0_FPS };
const SCALES = ['classic', 'hybrid', 'true'];

const repo = process.cwd();
const bench = path.join(repo, 'scripts/bench.mjs');
const step0 = path.join(repo, '.cache/step0');
const args = process.argv.slice(2);
const flag = (name) => args.find((a) => a.startsWith(`--${name}`));
const plain = args.filter((a) => !a.startsWith('--'));
const cycles = Number(plain[0] ?? 4);
const presets = plain.length > 1 ? plain.slice(1) : ['low', 'medium', 'high', 'ultra'];
const scales = (flag('scale')?.split('=')[1] ?? 'true,classic').split(',');
for (const s of scales) if (!SCALES.includes(s)) throw new Error(`unknown scale ${s} (${SCALES.join(', ')})`);
for (const p of presets) if (!(p in GATES)) throw new Error(`unknown preset ${p} (${Object.keys(GATES).join(', ')})`);
/** Every measured run: a preset in a scale, labelled "scale/preset". */
const labels = scales.flatMap((s) => presets.map((p) => `${s}/${p}`));
const query = (label) => {
  const [s, p] = label.split('/');
  return `?speedtest=1&preset=${p}&scale=${s}`;
};

if (flag('list')) {
  for (let c = 0; c < cycles; c++) {
    const order = labels.map((_, i) => labels[(i + c) % labels.length]);
    console.log(`cycle ${c + 1}: ${order.map((l) => `${l} ${query(l)}`).join(' | ')}`);
  }
  process.exit(0);
}

if (!existsSync(path.join(step0, 'dist/index.html'))) {
  if (!existsSync(step0)) execFileSync('git', ['worktree', 'add', '--detach', step0, STEP0_REF], { stdio: 'inherit' });
  execFileSync('npm', ['ci', '--no-audit', '--no-fund'], { cwd: step0, stdio: 'inherit' });
  execFileSync('npm', ['run', 'build'], { cwd: step0, stdio: 'inherit' });
}

const runs = [];
function run(dir, label, q) {
  let txt = '';
  try { txt = execFileSync('node', [bench, dir, label.replace('/', '-'), q], { encoding: 'utf8', timeout: 300_000 }); }
  catch (e) { txt = String(e.stdout ?? '') + String(e); }
  const fps = Number((txt.match(/Full quality, no cap: ([\d.]+)/) ?? [])[1]);
  const m = txt.match(/idle 1s: (\d+) draw calls, (\d+) tris over (\d+) rAFs/);
  const frames = m ? Math.min(Number(m[3]), 30) : NaN; // ~30 rendered frames at the cap
  const world = (txt.match(/World scale: (\w+)/) ?? [])[1] ?? '?';
  const rec = { label, world, fps, draws: m ? Number(m[1]) / frames : NaN, tris: m ? Number(m[2]) / frames : NaN };
  runs.push(rec);
  console.log(JSON.stringify(rec));
}

run(step0, 's0', '');
for (let c = 0; c < cycles; c++) {
  const order = labels.map((_, i) => labels[(i + c) % labels.length]);
  for (let i = 0; i < order.length; i += 2) {
    run(repo, order[i], query(order[i]));
    if (order[i + 1]) run(repo, order[i + 1], query(order[i + 1]));
    run(step0, 's0', '');
  }
}

const median = (xs) => { const s = [...xs].sort((a, b) => a - b); const k = s.length >> 1; return s.length % 2 ? s[k] : (s[k - 1] + s[k]) / 2; };
let failed = false;
console.log(`\nStep 0 fps this session: ${runs.filter((r) => r.label === 's0').map((r) => r.fps).join(', ')}`);
for (const label of labels) {
  const gate = GATES[label.split('/')[1]];
  const ratios = [], draws = [], tris = [];
  runs.forEach((r, i) => {
    if (r.label !== label) return;
    const s0 = runs[i - 1]?.label === 's0' ? runs[i - 1] : runs[i + 1]?.label === 's0' ? runs[i + 1] : null;
    if (s0) { ratios.push(r.fps / s0.fps); draws.push(r.draws); tris.push(r.tris); }
  });
  const med = median(ratios);
  // Draw calls stay within the 40-call budget (visual-quality-brief.md) in every scale.
  const drawMax = Math.max(...draws);
  const pass = med >= gate && drawMax <= 40;
  failed ||= !pass;
  console.log(`${label.padEnd(15)} median ${med.toFixed(3)} gate ${gate.toFixed(3)} ${pass ? 'PASS' : 'FAIL'}  ratios ${ratios.map((x) => x.toFixed(2)).join(' ')}  draws ≤ ${drawMax.toFixed(0)}  tris ≤ ${(Math.max(...tris) / 1000).toFixed(0)}k`);
}
process.exit(failed ? 1 : 0);
```

### Task 16: Run the performance gates (no code unless a gate fails)

**Files:**
- Modify only if a gate fails: `src/surface/worldScale.ts`, `src/surface/leafScale.ts` (the cuts below)

Spec 4.6: the gates must pass in True (the default) and Classic; Hybrid is benchmarked once on High and reported. Draw calls stay at 40 or fewer, with no shadow maps, post passes or extra render targets.

- [ ] **Step 1: Run the gates with nothing else running**

Close other heavy apps and make sure no other agent is running (CLAUDE.md: benchmark with only one agent; this Mac is often busy). About 4 minutes per cycle per scale, so about 32 minutes:

```bash
npm run build && node scripts/perf-gates.mjs 4 2>&1 | tee .cache/gates-v1.txt
```

Expected: the last eight lines read `true/low`, `true/medium`, `true/high`, `true/ultra`, `classic/low` … each with `PASS` (median ratio at or above its gate: Low 1.089, Medium 0.871, High 0.741, Ultra 0.610) and `draws ≤` 40 or fewer. Classic's ratios should match Step 1a's within noise (±5%).

- [ ] **Step 2: Benchmark Hybrid once on High**

```bash
node scripts/perf-gates.mjs 2 --scale=hybrid high 2>&1 | tee .cache/gates-v1-hybrid.txt
```

Record its median ratio and draws for Task 18 (a pass is expected but not required by the spec).

- [ ] **Step 3: Only if a True line fails: cut, in this order, re-running just the failing presets after each cut**

Re-run with `node scripts/perf-gates.mjs 4 --scale=true <preset>`. Record every cut and its numbers for Task 18.

1. **Leaf counts.** Add a trim to the table. In `src/surface/worldScale.ts`, in `interface ScaleSpec`, after `readonly distantCount: number;` add:

   ```ts
     /** Performance cut (spec 4.6): leaf counts times this, after the area rule. */
     readonly leafTrim: number;
   ```

   give `classic` and `hybrid` `leafTrim: 1,` and `true` `leafTrim: 0.85,` (each next to `distantCount`), and in `src/surface/leafScale.ts` replace

   ```ts
     return n > 0 ? Math.max(1, Math.round(n * leafCountScale(spec))) : 0;
   ```

   with

   ```ts
     return n > 0 ? Math.max(1, Math.round(n * leafCountScale(spec) * spec.leafTrim)) : 0;
   ```

   Then `npx vitest run` (the worldScale test's `scaledLeafCount(1500, SCALE_SPECS.true)` expectation becomes `Math.round(1500 * f * 0.85)`: update that one line to multiply by `SCALE_SPECS.true.leafTrim`). If it still fails, try `0.7`.
2. **Clutter counts.** In `SCALE_SPECS.true`, `clutter: 24` → `16` (then `12`). Tests read the count from the table.
3. **Distant-tree detail.** In `SCALE_SPECS.true`, `distantCount: 14` → `8`.

If a Classic line fails, it is noise or a regression from this plan: re-run that preset (`--scale=classic <preset>`) twice; if it still fails, compare against `main` with `node scripts/bench.mjs` and investigate before cutting anything (Classic must stay Step 1a's world).

- [ ] **Step 4: Commit any cut**

Skip this step when every gate passed. Otherwise run the checks and commit with the message for the cut you made (one commit per cut):

```bash
npx tsc --noEmit && npx vitest run && npm run verify:build && npm run test:e2e
git add src/surface/worldScale.ts src/surface/leafScale.ts tests/unit/worldScale.test.ts
git commit -m "perf(scale): trim True's leaf counts to pass the gates" -m "Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>"
# or: "perf(scale): fewer clutter items in True to pass the gates"
# or: "perf(scale): fewer distant trees in True to pass the gates"
```

**Files:**
- Modify: `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-step1b-world-scale-and-first-bite-design.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/codev/README.md`, `docs/superpowers/visual-quality-brief.md`

Replace:

```markdown
## Coming next: V1 · True Size

### New

```

with:

```markdown
## Coming next: V1 · True Size

### New
- **World scale:** a new button on the Start screen. Pick Classic, Hybrid or True and see the
  forest at a different size next to your worm.
- **True size** is the new normal. Leaves, mushrooms, rocks, ferns, the fallen log and the trees
  are as big as they really are next to a 15 cm earthworm. One oak leaf can cover most of the worm!
- Trees, rocks and the fallen log are solid now. Your worm slides along them instead of crawling
  through them.
- When a tree or rock gets in the way, the camera moves in closer so you can still see your worm.
- The Start screen shows which version you are playing: V1 · True Size.
- The speed test now says which world scale it used.

### Fixed
- Leaves no longer shake when your worm crawls under them.
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Record Part A as built in the spec**

In `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-step1b-world-scale-and-first-bite-design.md`:

Replace (in 4.4):

```markdown
- **Clear paths:** in every mode, props stay clear of the speed test's auto-drive loop and
  corridor, the spawn and Level 1's zones (5.5). Tests check this for each mode.
```

with:

```markdown
- **Clear paths:** in every mode, props stay clear of the speed test's auto-drive loop and
  corridor, the spawn and Level 1's zones (5.5). Tests check this for each mode. *(V1: Level 1
  does not exist yet, so its zones are checked in Part B's plan.)*
```

Then add a new subsection at the end of section 4, after 4.6 (before `## 5. Part B`), filling the two gate tables from `.cache/gates-v1.txt` and `.cache/gates-v1-hybrid.txt` (Task 16):

```markdown
### 4.7 As built in V1 · True Size

Built by `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-03-v1-true-size.md`. What differs from 4.1 to 4.6, and
what was measured.

- **Layouts.** Classic is Step 1a's world byte for byte (checksum tests on its trees, clutter,
  litter depth and meshes).
  - Hybrid: 5 trees (seed 15, at least 12.5 from the spawn), 5 rocks, the log at (9, 9) turned
    −0.6 and 19.8 long.
  - True: 2 trees in the corners (seed 284, at least 18 from the spawn), 5 rocks, and a 39.6-long
    log across the view at z = 15.5 that runs off both edges of the world; its top stands 4.9 up.
  - Both: 24 clutter items with at least 3 of each kind (a ×7 fern rarely finds room at random).
    Distant ring: Hybrid 24 trees ×4.5, True 14 trees ×9.
- **Trees.** Height, girth, lean, limbs, peeling curls, root collar and crown all scale; so does
  the bark pattern (otherwise a True birch log reads as a picket fence).
- **Solid footprints** are measured from the drawn meshes: trunk root flare 0.52 (birch) and 1.08
  (maple) per unit of trunk size, 0.95 for a birch clump, rocks 1.2 × their size, the log 1.15 × its
  radius plus 0.18 at its broken ends. Hero leaves use them too (Step 1a's were smaller than the
  drawn props). True's maple footprint is about 24 units across.
- **Classic's straight run.** Step 1a's maple at (1.9, 8.3) keeps its place; its root flare reaches
  about 0.15 into the far end of the spawn's straight run. Hybrid and True keep it clear by
  distance.
- **Camera.** Three sight lines (±0.5 rad), it stops 0.35 short, eases in at 5/s and out at 2.5/s,
  and is never nearer than 0.15 to a tall prop. Measured: at most 0.14 m per step, step-to-step
  change at most 0.05 (a hard turn alone: 0.08).
- **Disturbance map.** 96 × 96 cells over 12 m (12.5 cm cells as before), reach 3.65, on-top reach
  0.2. Each mode stamps only out to its biggest leaf plus the drape's foot: Classic 1.6 m (as in
  Step 1a), True 3.4 m. Dev Mac: True's stamping costs 0.22 ms a step against 0.05.
- **Big leaves lift level** (a lid) from radius 1.3, fully by 2.0; only True's leaves are that big.
  A tilted leaf clipped a worm curving under it (9 of more than 10,000 checks, up to 5 mm).
- **Leaf sizes.** Litter, detail and hero leaves all scale by the species' factor: the litter is
  real length on average, while detail and hero leaves keep their Classic share of it (smaller
  than real; a deviation from 4.5). Counts follow 1 / silhouette area. True draws 395 litter leaves
  on every preset; detail 237 / 264 / 422 and hero 3 / 6 / 8 on Medium / High / Ultra.
- **Hero leaves over 1 m** tilt and settle at rates divided by their length cubed. Part 0's jitter
  tests in True, same thresholds: litter 0.02% of frames turn back by more than 0.5 px (p99
  0.26 px); hero leaves 2.8% by more than 2 mm (p99 5.3 mm). Hero leaves rest a median 0.13 m above
  the body in True (Classic 0.10).
- **Not scaled:** Ultra's near clutter (its shader is bounded by NEAR_CLUTTER_REACH); it reads as
  young sedge and mushroom buttons.
- **Switching.** A pick is built 400 ms after the last pick; leaving the screen builds a waiting
  pick at once. The status reads "Building the forest…", then "The forest is ready: True." The new
  forest compiles while the old one still holds its programs, so a switch compiles no shaders (the
  e2e test counts `compileShader` calls).
- **Start:** Crawl, World scale, Graphics, Speed test (Play and Free crawl come with Part B). The
  speed-test report names the world scale.
- **Settings:** `scale` added to `wormsim.settings` (still version 1); every write keeps fields it
  does not know.
- **Known limits** (screenshots in `docs/codev/versions/v01-true-size/`):
  - big leaves lift level over the worm in True, so the lid look shows more than in Step 1a;
  - near a windblown pile's centre a few True leaves dip into the mound (the piles are no bigger
    than the leaves);
  - True looks a little barer: the same total leaf area as fewer, bigger leaves (a scratch measure
    of the litter alone: 44% of the floor covered against Classic's 50%);
```
